# FileCat 1.0.0 — current issue register

Updated 2026-10-31. **557 Remediated preliminarily, two Closed for preliminary scope, 19 unresolved statuses; 578 total.**
These are issue statuses, not remaining test counts. Subsequent scope closures require their own linked evidence; consolidation itself did not promote a status.
Remediated does not mean complete candidate qualification. All original findings, comparison results, commits, failure details and closure limits remain in the [frozen full register](FILECAT_1_0_RELEASE_ISSUE_HISTORY_20261006.md).

Use the [execution dashboard](FILECAT_1_0_RELEASE_EXECUTION_REPORT.md) for the remaining campaign checklist and the [blocker register](FILECAT_1_0_RELEASE_BLOCKERS.md) for required decisions/resources.

<a id="summary"></a>

## Remediated preliminarily

Earlier preliminary remediations (I001–I299) retain their detailed rows and evidence in the [archive](FILECAT_1_0_RELEASE_ISSUES_I001_I299.md). The index keeps existing issue anchors; recent detailed rows follow it.

| ID | Finding |
|---|---|
| <a id="i01"></a>I01 | Advertised private security-reporting route was disabled (High, operational). Remediated preliminarily 2026-10-28 under DEC-08: GitHub private vulnerability reporting is enabled (API reads `enabled: true`, `false` just before), and the owner is the named responder for SECURITY.md's 7-day acknowledgement and 14-day assessment. [Record](evidence/E-DEC-2026-10-28-owner-decisions.md); ledger case REL-REPORTING. |
| <a id="i05"></a>I05 | Media/record claims exceeded the implementation (Medium, contract). Remediated preliminarily 2026-10-29 under DEC-05: every advertised D-56 filesystem-record field is implemented and tested on its platform (Windows `$ObjId`/`$Reparse` entries, attribute runs, EFS users, Cloud Files placeholders and the full non-administrator notice; ext4 inode checksums and `i_version`; macOS FAT/exFAT entries and the HFS+ journal), and the public claims now say what is read. No media player is promised: the plan's audio/video row is metadata only. Hosted CI of the new tests and candidate qualification remain. [Record](evidence/E-I05-D56-implementation.md), [field audit](evidence/E-I05-D56-field-audit.md) |
| <a id="i09"></a>[I09](FILECAT_1_0_RELEASE_ISSUES_I001_I299.md#i09) | Recovery whole-source safety: FileCat wrote to its own folders on the disk being recovered after only a warning; destinations behind loop devices, disk images, VHDs and shares served by the same computer were taken for other disks |
| <a id="i14"></a>I14 | RAR decoder provenance / OSI-only eligibility (High, license/signing blocker). Remediated preliminarily 2026-10-29 under EXT-02, with RAR fully accessible. FileCat reads RAR with its own reader (`e57ebaaf`): RAR 2.0–7 ported from rardecode (BSD-2-Clause), RAR 1.5 from XADMaster (LGPL-2.1-or-later) in its own assembly. It ships SharpCompress as its own build, cut down to 7z, bzip2, xz and zstd (`ca293815`): no UnRAR-derived code, every part OSI-licensed, and notices, SBOM and packaging pins matching. Byte-exact against the retired path on every corpus archive; 1,380,000 RAR fuzz rounds and 16,000 archive fuzz rounds without a failure; packages built and checked on Windows, Ubuntu and macOS. Hosted CI of the pushed source and candidate qualification remain. [RAR reader](evidence/E-I14-rar-reader.md), [SharpCompress](evidence/E-I14-sharpcompress-osi.md) |

| <a id="i82"></a>[I82](FILECAT_1_0_RELEASE_ISSUES_I001_I299.md#i82) | Compare: the window said "1 difference" over a list of two (changed lines, then lines only on one side) |
| <a id="i83"></a>[I83](FILECAT_1_0_RELEASE_ISSUES_I001_I299.md#i83) | Find: a saved time range shown again in the dialog lost the last minute of its end day |
| <a id="i84"></a>[I84](FILECAT_1_0_RELEASE_ISSUES_I001_I299.md#i84) | Compare directories: of two names differing only in letter case one was dropped unseen; a size or time a listing does not give counted as the same |
| <a id="i85"></a>[I85](FILECAT_1_0_RELEASE_ISSUES_I001_I299.md#i85) | Count: a folder deleted and made again, or replaced, while its size was counted took the first folder's size as counted |

| <a id="i87"></a>[I87](FILECAT_1_0_RELEASE_ISSUES_I001_I299.md#i87) | Folder watch: a folder that kept changing was not read again until the changes stopped |
| <a id="i88"></a>[I88](FILECAT_1_0_RELEASE_ISSUES_I001_I299.md#i88) | Count: a folder's count went on after its tab closed and threw on every progress post, enough for the crash guard to end FileCat; leaving the folder kept the tab "counting" |
| <a id="i89"></a>[I89](FILECAT_1_0_RELEASE_ISSUES_I001_I299.md#i89) | Change journal: a journal that wrapped twice while it was read failed the whole read; entries skipped once were skipped silently |
| <a id="i90"></a>[I90](FILECAT_1_0_RELEASE_ISSUES_I001_I299.md#i90) | Find: appending a search from a root typed in another letter case listed each common file twice |
| <a id="i91"></a>[I91](FILECAT_1_0_RELEASE_ISSUES_I001_I299.md#i91) | Analyze folder: an analysis went on after its tab left the folder (then labelled and re-sorted the next folder) or closed (then failed reading the released listing) |
| <a id="i92"></a>[I92](FILECAT_1_0_RELEASE_ISSUES_I001_I299.md#i92) | Quick search: a miss walks every name on the window's thread in large listings |
| <a id="i93"></a>[I93](FILECAT_1_0_RELEASE_ISSUES_I001_I299.md#i93) | Duplicates: two names of one file (a hard link, a path through a junction) were grouped as copies, so "all but one" could mark the file itself for deletion; a link to a file was grouped with its target |
| <a id="i94"></a>[I94](FILECAT_1_0_RELEASE_ISSUES_I001_I299.md#i94) | Synchronize: folders inside each other (also through a junction) were offered for synchronizing; Mirror toward the outer one removed the source as an item only in the target |
| <a id="i95"></a>[I95](FILECAT_1_0_RELEASE_ISSUES_I001_I299.md#i95) | Signatures: a good OpenPGP signature without a trust line (gpg.conf trust-model always) read as good, for any key in the keyring |

| I493 | <a id="i493"></a>An elevated FileCat gave its hostile-input worker an enabled Administrators SID and every administrator privilege | Potential Critical (I08 containment; hostile input read anything administrators may read) | Must fix (I08/V10/TV-16) | Remediated preliminarily: exact `3d338fc9` lowered only the integrity label of a duplicated elevated token, so the worker kept enabled `S-1-5-32-544` and all 24 privileges including `SeDebugPrivilege`/`SeBackupPrivilege`; Windows' own `AccessCheck` granted it `FILE_GENERIC_READ` on an administrators-only file and a started worker read that file. The token is now taken from the UAC-filtered token where one exists, restricted with `DISABLE_MAX_PRIVILEGE` and deny-only privileged groups, lowered to low integrity and **verified before use**, and the job denies the worker FileCat's own USER handles. Four controls change from 0P/4F to 4P/0F; the elevated host, a normal VM session and an elevated signed-in VM session pass 14P/0F each. [Later-source hosted x64/ARM64 controls](evidence/E-CI-I493-I494-worker-identity.md) pass thirteen worker-boundary rows each. Actual verified sandboxing, unusual token constructions, Administrator Protection and installed candidate remain. | [Worker authority](evidence/E-I493-worker-administrator-authority.md) |
| I494 | <a id="i494"></a>The Unix picture worker decoded hostile input with root rights | Potential Critical (I08 containment; a codec flaw reaches the whole computer) | Must fix (I08/V10/TV-16) | Remediated preliminarily: a root-started FileCat handed pictures to a decoder running as uid 0, and the Linux seccomp filter in that worker denies process creation only, not file access. The worker now drops supplementary groups, group and user to the requesting sudo account before reading a byte, verifies the drop including that root cannot be regained, and decodes nothing when it is root with no requesting user. Ubuntu 26.04 probe runs show 0:0→1000:1000 with the root-only file refused, an unchanged ordinary user, and a refusal where the same authority would have read that file. The [Mac root probe](evidence/E-I494-mac-root-worker-identity.md) now verifies 0:0→501:20, irreversible root loss, denied root-only read and no-requester refusal at exact `8fe93efe`. The actual Mac worker also decodes PNG under sudo with ordinary-user UID and byte-identical output, while no-requester root refuses before input. Installed UI/consent, verified sandboxing, pkexec/doas/root-login, containers and candidate remain. | [Unix worker root identity](evidence/E-I494-unix-worker-root-identity.md) |
| I495 | <a id="i495"></a>A recovery source reported bytes it could not serve, so a direct and a helper read agreed on two equally short reads | Medium-High (validation reliability; the I110 acceptance risk, on real hardware) | Must fix (V09/I110) | Remediated preliminarily: on the authorized USB stick both paths returned **904 of the last 5,000 bytes** of the volume and compared equal. `IOCTL_DISK_GET_LENGTH_INFO` reports the partition's 7,795,113,984 bytes while the volume device serves only 7,795,109,888 - the size Windows reports for the file system - and plain `CreateFile`/`ReadFile` behave the same, while the physical disk serves those bytes. `DeviceTopology.ServedLength` now probes the end at open and both the direct source and the installed helper report what the device will serve; three unit controls and the on-stick direct/helper comparison (full 5,000 bytes through both paths) pass. Other devices, file systems and an installed candidate remain. | [USB source attribution](evidence/E-I495-usb-source-write-attribution.md) |
| I496 | <a id="i496"></a>A recovery session accepted an impossible drive description from its helper | Medium-High (validation reliability at a trust boundary) | Must fix (I17/I106/V06) | Remediated preliminarily: `PipeDeviceSource` stored the helper's reported length and sector size unchecked, although nothing else in the session can corroborate that answer and the length becomes the scan's bounds. A fake helper over a real pipe shows seven descriptions accepted — length 0, -1 and `long.MaxValue`, sector sizes 0, -4096, 3 and 1 MiB — now all refused, with `BrokeredDeviceSource` covered by the same constructor. Eight controls, seven failing before the fix; Platform suite 452P/36S. Installed-helper end-to-end and candidate remain. | [Helper drive description](evidence/E-I496-helper-drive-description.md) |

| I557 | <a id="i557"></a>The 7z and compressed-TAR readers decoded the members they pass without the expansion limit | High (availability: a small archive costs unbounded CPU) | Must fix (I08/V20) | Remediated preliminarily at `3a63099e`. To open a 7z member, SharpCompress decoded every earlier entry, even in other folders, with no limit. Compressed-TAR listing checked 1000:1 only between members, after a whole member was decompressed, and opening skipped members unchecked; cancellation was not observed inside a skip. Measured before: a 458,954-byte 7z decoded 2 GiB to open a 6-byte member, and a 1,710-byte tar.bz2 decoded 2 GiB just to list. Now the 7z reader passes entries itself, only those in the member's own folder, under 1000 times the archive's size (after 64 MiB) and cancelable. The TAR stream checks 1000:1 on every read (after 1 GiB) and observes cancellation. Four of six new tests fail on the old code. | [E-I557](evidence/E-I557-archive-pass-budget.md) |
| I558 | <a id="i558"></a>A 7z member read only in part shifted the next member of its folder | High (correctness: a healthy archive reported damaged, and wrong bytes before `34bc2abb`) | Must fix (V20) | Remediated preliminarily at `3a63099e`. SharpCompress did not finish an entry FileCat opened but did not read to its end, so the next entry of the folder started inside it. Quick view or F3 on a large member of a solid 7z followed by the next member gave a CRC-32 mismatch, and silently wrong bytes before the member check of `34bc2abb`. The reader now finishes the handed-out entry, under the same limit, before moving on within its folder. | [E-I557](evidence/E-I557-archive-pass-budget.md#i558--a-7z-member-read-only-in-part-shifted-the-next-member-of-its-folder-high-correctness) |
| I559 | <a id="i559"></a>The RAR-SEC-001 correction capped a solid RAR's run at 8 GiB | High (correctness: a healthy solid RAR unreadable past a member over 8 GiB) | Must fix (V20) | Remediated preliminarily at `3bb3d995`. The security session's `a718c777` bounded the members a solid RAR passes, which is sound, but its run limit had no declared size and so took the 8 GiB cap of a member of unknown size, and it counted bytes already returned. Observed with an 8.5 GiB member then a 13-byte one (WinRAR 7.01, solid): the 13-byte member was refused after the big one was read whole, and when opened directly. The run is now bounded by its expansion ratio alone. Both cases read the 13 bytes, and all 232 RAR tests, Codex's bomb tests among them, pass. | [E-I557](evidence/E-I557-archive-pass-budget.md#i559--the-rar-correction-capped-a-solid-run-at-8-gib-high-correctness) |

A sharing violation there now gives a distinct in-use result. Copy, sync and extraction throw it inside their retry loop: the question says the item is in use, and Retry replaces it once the program lets go. ## Maintenance

Update the current row and dashboard when evidence changes. Keep existing IDs stable; assign the next unused ID to a new proved finding. Preserve severity/disposition unless evidence or an owner decision justifies a change.
Record new execution details once in the [activity log](FILECAT_1_0_RELEASE_ACTIVITY_LOG.md) and link the exact evidence. Frozen history files are not current state and must not be appended to.
All 113 legacy issue-detail headings retain matching anchors in the relevant current table rows.
